<meta>This page serves as a table of contents or a summary of key theological themes found in the subsequent manuscript, likely a pietist or mystical treatise influenced by Jacob Böhme.</meta>

# Observations on the Most Important Matters

<margin>p. 4.</margin> Whoever is not minded to follow his Savior and Redeemer Jesus Christ in all things, let him <note>NB: Latin 'nota bene', meaning 'note well'</note> leave these lines unread—because they would be of no help to him anyway.

<margin>the same page</margin> How one may touch and find the Highest Good.

<margin>p. 8. 9.</margin> That without the Holy Trinity <note>original: "heil: 3 Zahl", literally 'holy 3-number'</note> no true, blissful Divine Being is to be found—and whoever excludes the Son and Holy Spirit from it, does not have the Father as God either—and how one must not imagine a fierce and angry God within the Holy Trinity; rather, if the eternal creatures (who also consist of love and wrath) were to depart from love, then the darkness, or the wrath, would become manifest.

<margin>p. 15.</margin> Concerning the God-man, our Lord Jesus Christ; very beautiful.

<margin>p. 18.</margin> How we can become anointed ones <term>Gesalbete: a reference to 'Christians' as those sharing in the 'Christos' or Anointed One</term> and also brothers of our Lord Jesus, and how, according to p. 19 and 20, we must essentially live and reign royally in all three parts of man—and yet, for that reason, man cannot say: "I am Christ," as the poor D: <note>likely a reference to a specific person, possibly a radical sectarian or 'Deist'</note> did.

<margin>p. 22.</margin> The unwavering, essential indwelling of Christ shall alone be our goal.

<margin>p. 26.</margin> How Heavenly Wisdom is: a bride of the highest love—a wife of the Lamb of God—a queen of heaven, and regent of the earth—She is more beautiful than the moon, brighter than the sun—She is a Divine pearl, the wonderful stone <note>a reference to the 'Philosopher's Stone' used here in a spiritual sense</note> and priceless jewel of the wise—whoever has become a partaker of her, beholds God's wonders inwardly within himself—She teaches him how the world was created, etc.

<margin>p. 34.</margin> It is the duty of every believing soul to fight against sin and its origin, even unto blood—also that on p. 35, one should not attribute to God that He created some for salvation and others for damnation, but rather p. 36, God is Love.

<margin>p. 37.</margin> What a heavy judgment awaits the apostate prince <note>referring to Lucifer/Satan</note>, because in his intentional malice he still continues to do evil.

<margin>the same page</margin> What a glorious image man was before the Fall.

<margin>p. 38.</margin> Concerning the difference between the fall of the Devil and the fall of man—and that man, in his innermost being, has still retained a ray of the Divine light.

<summary>This page provides a thematic index to a mystical theological work, emphasizing the necessity of following Christ, the nature of the Trinity, and the role of Heavenly Wisdom. It highlights the internal spiritual struggle and the "Divine light" remaining in humanity despite the Fall.</summary>
<keywords>Trinity, Jesus Christ, Heavenly Wisdom, Sophia, Salvation, Fall of Man, Divine Light, Mysticism, Inner Life</keywords>

--- Page 18 ---
39. If a person follows the pull of God's grace, they would be born anew and regain the Divine Image—but if they follow the drawing impulse of Satan and the spirit of this world, they remain an animal and a carnal, beast-like person, etc.

p. 40. How it required an Incarnation <note>original: "Menschwerdung"; the theological concept of God becoming human in Jesus</note> to save humanity.

p. 42. Through the apostasy from the Gospel (or Christ), the Enemy has been placed into the greatest joy and power—(but it is his downfall).

p. 44. When Reason reaches its highest peak, it stands under the government of the spirit of Babylon—and since it cannot even grasp or comprehend the foremost mysteries, true wonders, and the riches of this visible world, how could it <insert>possibly</insert> want to comprehend spiritual and divine mysteries?

p. 46. Where God wishes to perform His divine teaching office (instructing), there He demands a New Heaven and a New Earth within the person—in which He wishes to entrust such a one with all the treasures of Wisdom.

p. 48. The excellence of Reason, for example, extends only to the maintenance of this transitory life—and the Fall of Adam and Eve was a cause of Reason. <margin>Note: And now people place Reason upon the throne, and thus wish to believe nothing more than what can be grasped with this dim light of Reason.</margin>

p. 49. Reason establishes and directs nothing but confusion and follies—in earthly as well as Heavenly things.

p. 50. Through Reason, the tower-builders of Babel would be a sign of Nimrod <note>A biblical figure associated with the Tower of Babel and earthly tyranny</note>, attempting to climb unhindered into Heaven with their old self—indeed, the whole body of sin and death—and thus the Incarnation of God would have been unnecessary.

p. 52. What disputes so-called "Sound Reason" has brought onto the path regarding the divine Incarnation of Jesus Christ.

p. 54. That those who follow Christ in the Rebirth <note>original: "Wiedergeburt"; the spiritual transformation of the believer</note> certainly attained: the Renewal of All Things.

p. 56. At the foundation of the highest degree of Reason lies the most subtle Antichrist <note>original: "Wieder-Christ"</note>—Reason and Evangelical faith are diametrically opposed to one another.

Note: The further continuation follows at the back.

<summary>This page outlines a mystical critique of human Reason, arguing that it is a product of the Fall and an obstacle to divine rebirth. The author contrasts the "dim light" of intellectual logic with the spiritual transformation required to understand God's mysteries.</summary>
<keywords>Reason vs. Faith, Incarnation, Rebirth, Tower of Babel, Nimrod, Babylon, Divine Image, spiritual transformation</keywords>

# Preliminary Reminder

...twenty thousand, in the victorious bravery of faith, must always be defeated by ten thousand, and patiently endure until the end.(a)

<margin>(a) The victory lies in perseverance.</margin>

But whoever is not minded to follow his Savior and Redeemer Christ Jesus in all things, and is not willing—without looking back—to take up the plow of true <term>Christianity</term> <note>German: Christenthum; here referring to the active practice of faith rather than just the religion</note>, let him leave these lines unread. In the meantime, let him examine his entire theology, or his composed doctrines of <term>godliness</term> <note>German: Gottseligkeit; a central Pietist term for living a life devoted to God</note>, to see if there is perhaps a true guide to be found therein on how one might **grope for** and find the Highest Good. <term>Acts 17:27</term> <note>The author references the biblical passage where Paul speaks of seeking God and perhaps reaching out for Him and finding Him.</note> For whoever lives with a perverse will and a disinclined spirit, in words and conduct, against his own orthodoxy,(b)

<margin>(b) With the mouth one indeed confesses orthodoxy; whoever teaches and—Note Well—lives differently than the Word of God teaches, profanes the name of God among us (from which may our dear Heavenly Father preserve us). But the heart does not perceive this, otherwise bitter wildings <note>original: "Härlinge"; sour, uncultivated grapes</note> and sour crab-apples would not grow every day on the outermost tree of religion.</margin>

he is a mocker of his formal faith, a despiser of the Law and the Prophets, without God in the world, and consequently entirely unfit to enter upon the narrow way of life.

But those souls who are weary, exhausted in their own ways, seeking grace, and heavy-laden, will easily perceive what comes from the God of Love, the Spirit of...

<summary>The author warns that true Christianity requires total commitment and perseverance, criticizing those who maintain "orthodox" doctrines but live contrary to God's Word. He invites weary, grace-seeking souls to continue reading while dismissing those who only practice a formal, hollow faith.</summary>

<keywords>perseverance, Savior, true Christianity, godliness, Acts 17:27, orthodoxy, hypocrisy, narrow way, grace</keywords>

# Chapter 1.
## Concerning the Divine Essence.

---

->§. I.<-

GOD is the highest Good, and the source of all that is good; spiritual, simple <note>In this context, "simple" refers to the theological concept of divine simplicity—being without parts or composition.</note>, unchangeable, immortal, pure Life, infinite, clean, holy, free, almighty, possessing all fullness, all-knowing, wise, pure Light, Justice, Truth, Goodness, Humility, Constancy, Peace and Rest, Joy and Refreshment; In whom all divine attributes are inseparably united. **GOD is love,** in whose most holy essence there is the most beautiful, unchangeable unity; And just as the loving GOD is all good and pure goodness; so also is His will and everything that accords with it.

§. 2. Now, when GOD the Most High, according to the pleasure of His will, flows out from His infinite and incomprehensible Divinity into revelation, the original first number of His Majestic, all-sufficient, most-praised essence proves itself **to be a Father.** In the second number, GOD is a Son of the Father, who equals the Father in all things; And in the third number, a Spirit of the Father and of the Son in this one divine essence.

<note>The author uses the term "number" to describe the persons of the Trinity, emphasizing the mathematical and mystical order of the Godhead.</note>

<summary>This page introduces the first chapter on the nature of God, defining the Divine as the ultimate source of all virtues and explaining the emergence of the Trinity—Father, Son, and Holy Spirit—from the infinite essence.</summary>
<keywords>Divine Essence, Trinity, Attributes of God, Divine Simplicity, Revelation, Father, Son, Holy Spirit</keywords>

# The 1st Chapter.

which is to be considered and noted in a divine manner, without regard for privilege, age, or time. This is because, in this revelation, the Father is not older or larger than the Son, and the Holy Spirit is not younger or smaller than the Father and the Son; for all three numbers <note>The author uses "numbers" (Zahlen) here in a mystical sense to refer to the Persons of the Trinity</note> of the Godhead have their root and ground in the infinite Divinity, where they exist without any beginning or end. Thus, although the Father truly and actually begot the Son, <margin>Yes, and still begets him.</margin> and the Spirit essentially proceeds from the Father and the Son; they are nevertheless the one, true, essential, and incomprehensible GOD, in a divine Trinity <note>original: "Dreyzahl"</note>, which always flows back into its own unity. From this it is evident that God is as much one as three, and three as one; and that he has begotten his Son today; most highly glorified and praised without end.

§. 3. Therefore, just as no true happiness <note>The German "Glückseligkeit" implies both happiness and eternal blessedness/salvation</note> is to be found outside of God, so also no blessed divine being is to be met with if someone were to exclude one of those three most holy numbers in the Godhead, or could rob it of another; for the first cannot be without the other, nor the second without the third. If someone were able to make the Son smaller than the Father, then the greatness in the Father that surpassed the Son would find itself eternally dissatisfied and without fulfillment. For in that case, so to speak, it would be deprived of its essential love and all-sufficient glory—

<summary>This page explores the co-equality and eternal nature of the Trinity, arguing that the Father, Son, and Holy Spirit are unified in power and age. It emphasizes that God's perfection depends on the absolute equality and mutual necessity of these three divine persons.</summary>
<keywords>Trinity, Godhead, Father, Son, Holy Spirit, Co-equality, Divine Unity, Blessedness, Eternal Generation</keywords>

# Of the Divine Essence.

<margin>
(a) This is very beautifully presented as the Father’s property, namely: fury, wrath, and sharpness; but just as in us, the creatures, when the Son’s nature—as the vessel of love—is found therein, then the love toward God and toward one another is so very much instilled in us.
</margin>

deprived of heart. If anyone wishes to imagine the Father <gloss>(a)</gloss> as a fierce or wrathful God, let him know that the Father never was, nor shall be, *without the Son, nor the Son without the Spirit;* for they are all three in one another without beginning and end, and yet one Beginner and Completer, Creator, Sustainer, Savior, and Restorer of all things, who *grasps and encloses everything within Himself.* And as long as anyone wishes to claim that the Holy Spirit cannot be God, as well as the Father and the Son, he may truly know that in that state and meanwhile, he has neither the Father nor the Son as God, and consequently does not know God; For if he knew God, he would also know the Holy Spirit <gloss>as Love</gloss> *in the Godhead*, but because he does not know the Holy Spirit, he does not know God, nor does he have eternal life, to which a person is accompanied and led by the Holy Spirit. And if the Holy Spirit were not as great a God as the Father, then the Son would be smaller *than the Father*, for the Father would be—according to the greatness by which he surpassed the Son—without the outflowing of the Holy Spirit. Since, then, the Divine Essence has been entirely and utterly blessed before and in all eternities, and remains unchangeable, then <margin>see Jacob Böhme <note>original: "vide Jac. B." Jacob Böhme (1575–1624) was a German Christian mystic whose work heavily influenced this text's view of the "wrathful" Father and "loving" Son.</note></margin> the Son is as great as the Father, and fills all the powers of the Father; likewise there goes forth a joyful [Spirit], of equal greatness and glory as

# Chapter 1.

the Father and Son, the most blessed and powerful Holy Spirit, flows out from all the powers of the Father through the powers of the Son. When we also contemplate this most holy, triune, and highest Good as revealed, we find that the Father from eternity has brought forth his eternal, embracing, and satisfying Heart—or Son—and that the Holy Spirit from eternity has proceeded from the Father through the Son as an infinite Spirit of Joy, filling the infinite depth and height of the entire divine Being.

§. 4. All of nature and all created things depict in their own way, or represent in obscurity, the highly blessed Trinity <note>original: "Dreyzahl"</note>—the Father, the Word, and the Holy Spirit—who are one in the most blessed essence of the Godhead. As an example, all natural things consist of three principles, namely Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury <note>These are the "tria prima" of alchemy, representing body, soul, and spirit in all matter</note>. Three distinct and united parts make up a human being: namely the spirit, soul, and body, which live, work, and move within one another in human form or shape.(a) But God the Creator is by no means to be confused with his creatures.

<margin>(a). How this trinity works within him, man cannot even explain within himself: how then, out of his separation <insert>or</insert> [fallen state], can he hope to grasp his great Creator and high Origin? Must he not, in doing so, become a visionary <note>original: "Fantast," referring to one lost in delusions</note> or a naturalist <note>one who explains the world only through physical laws, ignoring the divine</note>? S</margin>

§. 5. GOD Father, Son, and Holy Spirit, in his unrevealed essence, is without beginning and without end, whom no one has seen nor can see. In his very first revelation, he is a holy Trinity, triune within himself.

<summary>This page explores the internal relationship of the Trinity as a "Spirit of Joy" and argues that the three-fold nature of God is mirrored in the physical world through alchemical principles and the human makeup of body, soul, and spirit.</summary>
<keywords>Trinity, Holy Spirit, Tria Prima, Salt-Sulfur-Mercury, Body-Soul-Spirit, Divine Revelation, Alchemy, Theology</keywords>

# Chapter 1.

eternal Nothingness to all creatures. In his revealed eternity, he is a Father of spirits. In nature and those invisible worlds, he is—within a distinction of degrees, in his blessed emanations and assumed garments of glory—the highest Good. In the earthly, mixed world, he is both **the blessing, goodness, righteous adornment, and innermost light of life of all things, as well as a jealous God and a consuming fire.** <note>The author uses "mixed world" to describe the physical realm where good and evil, light and dark, coexist.</note> In the dark, anguished kingdom of death and the hells, however, he is an eternal Nothingness in his infinity, and a punishing judge in the wrath of his justice. In his sweet Son, our most precious Savior, he is **grace, reconciliation, righteousness, purification, sanctification, redemption, love, fortification, and a restorer of the entire fallen human race.**

§. 7. From eternity, the Father has begotten the Son as his eternal, embracing, and satisfying love, from both of whom the Spirit of joys proceeds. The Father is in the Son, the Son is in the Father, and the Holy Spirit is the joyful power itself, which proceeds from the Father through the Son by the embrace of the Son. When the Father desires, the Son embraces, and so the Spirit proceeds from the Father through the Son. The Father desires always and from eternity. The

<summary>This page describes the multifaceted nature of God across different realms—from the highest glory to the depths of hell—and explains the Trinitarian relationship as a process of eternal desire, embrace, and joyful emanation.</summary>

# Of the Divine Essence.

The Son shines always and from eternity in the 
desire of the Father. The Holy Spirit pro-

**the Love and the Light. The Father is an** 
**infinite power, the Son is an infinite** 
**power, the Holy Spirit is an infinite power.** 
What the Father wills, that the Son and 
the Holy Spirit also will; for they are **one in three, and** 
**three in one, in equal power and majesty;** 
Yet the one is not the other, although they 
are three in one. The Father loves the Son, 
the Son loves the Father, the Holy Spirit 
loves the Father and Son; for they are three 
in one. The sun of the Son shines from 
eternity and forevermore through the powers of 
the Father, which everywhere give a reflection 
in the Spirit.

deeper than all abysses, longer and broader than

close him. He fills and encompasses all, 
is inside and out, the Innermost and 
the Outermost. O how great and wonderful is our 
God, most highly praised and glorified eter-
nally! Amen.

## Chapter 2.

### Of the God-Man Christ JESUS.

§. 1. The emanated image of the most holy essence of the Godhead is an outflow of the original, infinite Wisdom. This self-subsisting essence of Wisdom, or the virginal Lamb of God—in which the Holy Trinity dwells as in its emanated divine eternal Spirit, Soul, Heart, and Body—is not improperly called **the primordial humanity, or God-Man**, which flows out into the seven spirits of God according to divine manner and quality. And just as this emanated essence may be called a Lamb of God, or the true image of the divine essence, as well as the Body, Tabernacle, and the City of God; so it can also, in the same sense, be called and regarded **as a garment of God**, with which he gloriously adorned and decorated himself before nature or any creature was brought into being or formed.

§. 2. Now, after man—who had been *created in the image of God* after the creation of the angels and the fall of those apostate angels—had been overpowered by the fallen angelic prince and had *lost* the divine image, a necessary consequence followed: the second Person of the Godhead <note>The Son, or the Word</note>, whose specific impression the first Adam bore, had to descend in His original and eternal humanity and become man. This was because the devil, by attacking and destroying the divine image in Adam, had come too close to the essential Son and His eternal humanity. Like a cruel rebel, he presented himself before the King of Life with murder and tyranny, intending to *drive Him out and annihilate Him* entirely <gloss>(a)</gloss>, so that the entire inheritance—the human race and the earthly sphere with everything in it—might become his prey and property. He had already *perpetrated* a similar act of murder within his own nature, and then carried it out in Adam to the best of his ability; he thus intended to continually practice and fully execute his evil design against the Son of God. Now, the Lord also saw clearly that the hostile nature and essence of Belial <note>A Hebrew term for the devil or a personification of worthlessness and wickedness</note> was entirely inclined toward crucifying, murdering, slaughtering, and killing. For wherever he, or his Spirit, does not represent his place, there surely reigns the Antichrist <note>original: "Wieder-Christ"</note> or his spirit. He will never allow his right of primogeniture <note>the legal and spiritual right of the firstborn</note> to be transferred to anyone else. His human person is so exalted that no one among all the children of men or the angels of God can bear such a union, or so close a kinship with the blessed Deity, as our God-Man Christ Jesus. One can therefore be assured that no other than the Antichrist and his members strive to be the deputies <note>original: "Stell-Verweser," meaning a substitute or vicar</note> of Christ or of Wisdom. *What Christ has done in his members on earth, still does, and henceforth will do until the end of the world, certainly belongs to him as the Head, who performs such things in and through them.* Therefore, it must no longer be I in my corrupted selfhood <term>Selbheit: the ego or fallen human nature</term>, *but Christ in the new creation, or the self-subsisting holy being,* according to all three parts of man, essential-

# Concerning the God-Man Jesus CHRIST.

Understanding everywhere in the renewed soul and body, which also possess an active and passive life within themselves, indeed in all parts and powers of man. # Chapter 3.

The highest, indescribable wisdom and the loveliness of God—unrecognized by all creatures—was first brought into revelation by the blessed Godhead and made comprehensible as a virginal Mother of Life. He possessed her both unrevealed and in the beginnings of His ways. In relation to God, she is entirely passive, like the moon reflecting the sun. She is a bride of the highest Love, and a wife of the Lamb of God <note>A reference to the "Marriage of the Lamb" from the Book of Revelation</note>. She is the beginner, nourisher, and perfecter of virtues; a Queen of Heaven and Regent of the Earth. One day, she will completely sweep away the manifold folly, raging godliness, and impetuous malice of the entire world with a broom. All things are formed within her, and God has instilled her into all who possess understanding; indeed, He has poured out His work everywhere. She is omnipresent and meets God-loving spirits on the right path of life, both internally and externally. Her faithful and proven lovers love everyone in God, and God in all things, but outside of Him, they love not a single one. She is the divine and gold-flaming tabernacle <term>Hütte: literally "hut" or "tabernacle," referring to the dwelling place of the Divine among humanity</term> of God with men; for the throne of God and of the Lamb shall be in her, and He who sits upon the throne will then dwell in and over them as if in a tabernacle. She is more beautiful than the moon, brighter than the sun and all the stars. She is a divine pearl and a brightly shining light.

# On Divine Wisdom, etc.

Light. The wonderful stone and inestimable jewel of the Sages <note>original: "Stein der Weisen" – the Philosopher's Stone, here used as a metaphor for Christ or spiritual perfection</note>. ## Chapter 4.

### Concerning the Fallen Angels and Evil Spirits.

§. 1. All spirits into whom God breathed life during the Creation possess, each one, their own individual will, and every will possesses its own specific desires. Now, it could not be otherwise: by all right and law, it must be required of every free will that there be a tempering of desires and a submission of that will under the will of God. This is a duty which the holy angels observed; they conquered and cast away all rising objections, just as is proper and fitting for obedient children. The apostates <note>Those who have abandoned their faith or principles; the fallen angels.</note>, however, did not merely neglect this, but—to all appearances—flowed out into resentment and riot against the one and only Supreme Will, first secretly as hidden mutineers, and subsequently openly as wicked rebels. Whereupon Wrath—as an immediate consequence of stubbornness and hostile opposition against the will of God—ignited within their own wills, threw the mind into confusion, and finally set the entire wheel of nature <note>term: wheel of nature (German: *Rad der Natur*). A concept often found in the mysticism of Jacob Böhme, referring to the cyclical, driving forces of manifestation and life.</note> into flames and hell. For when Wrath became active within the will, they did not betake themselves to seek Grace—which they still could have done in that state when the Wrath and Curse first made themselves felt, through a timely recognition of their faults, by means of

It describes the transition of these spirits from secret mutineers to open rebels, resulting in the ignition of "Wrath" and the creation of a hellish state within the "wheel of nature."</summary>

§. 2. That within this subordination, or the submission of the free will under the will of God, a desire manifested itself which asked: Why should I, who am free, depend <note>original: *dependiren*</note> upon another? Or why can I not be the most prominent among the wills of God, just as well as this one or that one? I say that reason can well conclude—without overstepping its bounds—that such thoughts, or the like, must have made themselves known among the angels in their majestic freedom. Reason can also imagine how easy it was for such free spirits and unconstrained wills either to annihilate or to cling to such stirrings, causes, and effects—which in and of themselves are neither evil, nor can be called so—even though the misuse of them may have been an inducement to evil. # Chapter 4.

They could also well have known that if the all-governing, all-wise *disposition* <note>original: "Disposition"; refers here to God's divine plan or arrangement of the universe</note> of God were willfully resisted, and His will were unjustly disparaged, nothing other than wrath, punishment, and rejection would follow.

§. 3. Our own experience teaches us how wrath increases when one becomes aware of one's sins, and yet—instead of seeking grace in a timely manner and surrendering one's deviations—one nevertheless arms oneself for resistance with excuses and justifications, wanting either to be seen as innocent or resorting to hypocrisy. How is not all grace and help, love and redemption hindered when a sinner unites himself with the fire of wrath and remains standing in the highest stubbornness? What kind of punishment does not follow a rebellious, constantly hardening sinner who, through his godless unbelief and wickedness, dares to resist and even to nullify the sole almighty Love? The fallen sons of God of the first world, *Jannes* and *Jambres* <note>According to tradition, these were the Egyptian sorcerers who opposed Moses</note>, *Pharaoh* with his *Egyptians*, *Absalom* and *Ahithophel*, the scribes, Pharisees, and chief priests, together with the Antichrist <note>original: "Wieder-Christ"</note> and all adversaries in the New Covenant, are clear outward images of the original *Belial-like* <term>Belial: a Hebrew term often used to personify wickedness or the devil</term> rebellion.

## Chapter 5.
### Concerning the Image of God and the Fall of Man from God.

§. 1. Before the Fall <note>original: "Abfall," referring to the lapse from grace</note>, man stood in a wondrous, perfect harmony and a harmonious stretching forth of each and every power toward God, as his origin and highest blessedness; as well as in an essential flowing-in of the blessed God and highest Good into all the powers of man, serving as their nourishment, ornament, beauty, and glory. The image of God shone brightly in these lovely humans, and the crown of this glorious image was grounded in the unconstrained freedom of the eternal will and mind, which also, in a creaturely fashion, made him like the Creator. In short, God gave Adam everything that was fitting for him, and supplemented with the flowing-in of His divine goods everything that he lacked. Therefore, the greater and nobler the treasure, † the heavier and more sorrowful is the loss of the same.

§. 6. It is remarkable that the wickedness—which had crept in as a small, devouring fire-worm or hellish salamander within the cunning serpent-beast—has, through the deceit of sins and the deviation of men from God, subsequently strengthened and increased so much. It has become like an all-consuming dragon, a devouring beast, a universal deceiver, and a mother of all kinds of harlotries, harlots, and whoremongers, which has swallowed, eaten up, seduced, and enchanted the entire inhabited earth with its kingdoms and empires, making it an enemy of the one true God.

# Chapter 5

§. 7. The covenant and promise established with man after the Fall in Paradise—which the first world, in its godlessness, subsequently brought to ruin—God renewed through Noah. Afterward, He called Abraham and confirmed the promise of Christ, gave the Law through Moses, and finally came Himself with the grace and truth of the Gospel <note>original: "Evangelii"</note>. All that preceded was not able to displace the Enemy in such strength as did the apostasy from the Gospel; for through this, wickedness has gained many heads, horns, and victory-wreaths throughout all of nature. These the Lord will annihilate with the revelation of His coming, so that the entire "man of sin" <note>A biblical reference to the personification of evil or the Antichrist</note> must cease at his very foundation, and all things must be restored. Amen! Yes, come Lord Jesus—

§. 8. Oh, that the world would for once reflect and abandon its mockery! So that its bonds would not become even harder, which are certainly already far too hard. (Isaiah 28:22.)

§. 9. In general, let everyone be convinced that our gracious God and dear Father provided for the ruin of the human race in the wisest and most tender manner; indeed, His divine providence had so ordained it, that if man did not remain steadfast in his first undivided state, he could then be divided. ## Chapter 6.
### Concerning the Reason and Self-Wisdom of Earthly Men.

<margin>(a) From this, the Free-thinkers and Naturalists <note>Naturalists: those who believe only in natural laws and reject supernatural revelation.</note> have arisen, because they refuse to accept anything except what they can grasp with their reason, even though all spiritual and divine things reach far beyond the narrow district of reason—indeed! almost all teachers remain within their reason, in which they also explain the Holy Scripture—therefore</margin>

§. 1. In these current final days of this world's course, people conceive for themselves such a reason as has never been, nor will ever be found; and consequently, a harmful way of speaking has arisen, which keeps many honest souls away from the path of faith, and causes offense to those already walking upon it. It is roughly of this content: Christianity, or the mystery of faith and the Gospel, are indeed above reason, but not contrary to it; therefore, one must use one's reason and not abuse it, and consequently reject everything that is against sound reason.(a) We wish, accordingly, to weigh and briefly consider reason, both in its finest and best condition (which some call an enlightened and eternal reason), as well as in its coarsest and middle kind:

§. 2. §. 15. Carnal reason is governed by animality <note>original: "Thierlichkeit"; refers to the lower, instinctive nature of man</note>, and is subjected to its will and senses. Refined pagan reason is grounded in humanity, and lies beneath its passions. Virtuous and honorable people, with their reason, are disposed by the natural law, upon which Moses and the Prophets depend; they do unto others everything they wish others to do unto them. But what they do not want done to themselves, they also do not do to others, and they then take heed of the most important parts of the law, such as legal judgment, mercy, and faith. But when **Evangelical Grace** begins, the entire legalistic nature is gradually **swallowed up** by the righteousness of God, which is through the faith of Christ.

# Chapter 6.

<margin>(a) From this comes the noun: their scholars (in reason) are so darkened (namely, in divine things)</margin>
The truth of this is confirmed in small children and very elderly people. Reason is more inclined toward **naturalism and atheism**<gloss>(a)</gloss> than toward godliness; because all eternal and invisible things, which exceed its sphere, appear to it as foolish and fantastical; indeed, to reason and its coarse nature, they are nothing but nullities. Therefore, **reason—despite all its chatter about God, faith, and salvation—is and remains a faithful companion of dark, desperate unbelief**, until its complete death, dissolution, and destruction, or the restoration of the fallen human being. All of this is proven and brought to light by the lovers of reason through their teachings and their lives.

§. 18. Yet it must be known that reason is a gift of God; for man in his fallen state could not handle or understand a greater gift. One should not think that the World-Spirit <note>The "Welt-Geist" or Spirit of the World refers here to the animating force of the physical, temporal universe as opposed to the Holy Spirit.</note> could have produced human reason without the cooperation of God. Rather, just as God made garments of skins for the fallen body <note>A reference to Genesis 3:21, where God clothes Adam and Eve after the Fall.</note>, He also worked through the World-Spirit to create reason along with the rational soul as a clothing for the poor spirit, which, together with its eternal soul, had become entirely misshapen through the Fall. Therefore, reason—like all other mixed essences <term>essences: the fundamental spiritual or physical substances that make up a being</term> of this world—must necessarily be dissolved. Unfortunately, there are people who strip away reason to their own curse,

# Chapter 6.

all of their unrest and unbelief are overwhelmed, taken captive, and kept well-locked under the custody of faith until the day of execution <note>The author uses a legal metaphor here: just as a criminal is held until their sentence is carried out, the "unrest" of the mind is restrained by faith until the final judgment.</note>.

§. 22. The atheist says: Convince me with natural reasons that there is a **God**. Answer: Horses, mules, and other irrational animals that have no understanding cannot be convinced of **God** and His essence; a rational human being, however, is easily led to the knowledge of the Creator. For if the first human had been the cause of his own being and existence, then he would still be found today, and all his descendants would likewise be present. Experience, however, proves that all things in this course of the world have their beginning, middle, and end. It follows, therefore, without compulsion, that they were made or created by someone.

§. 23. Yes, he says: They have their origin in nature, and disappear back into the same. Answer: It follows then from this, that there is a Nature-**God**. If that is affirmed, then one who gropes after the unknown **God** <note>original: "unbekannten Gott"</note> (Acts 17:27) <note>A reference to the Apostle Paul's sermon at the Areopagus in Athens, where he speaks of the "Unknown God."</note> can find that He exists as easily as one concludes from the clothes of a man or the work of a master that the man and maker himself must be present, even if one does not see him personally.

# Of Reason and One's Own etc.

§. 24. To this, an objection might again follow: "Yes, it is the World-Spirit <note>original: *Welt-Geist*; likely referring to the *Anima Mundi* or a pantheistic vital force in nature</note>." Answer: That the World-Spirit, or the mover of nature, is not without a beginning or is not God Himself, is clearly evident from its effects and natural offspring, all of which—just as much as the driver of nature—have their beginning, middle, and end. For whatever the work is, such must the cause necessarily be; it follows therefore, without force, that the World-Spirit is not its own creator, and consequently must have a **Master**, as surely as the **pot** has its **potter**, who can break the one and make another out of it. Now, when reason has come this far, then the beginning of faith in an earnest seeker will transcend the circle of nature, and several worlds that are invisible will meet him through faith, which enclose and encompass the entire visible creation; For through faith we understand that the courses of the world were prepared by God's word, so that the things which are seen have come from those which do not appear. <note>A paraphrase of Hebrews 11:3</note> Or: In faith our spirit understands that the worlds were compacted <note>original: *compactirt*; meaning joined firmly together or integrated</note> or joined into one another by God's word, and the visible things were made from the invisible. # Chapter 7.

§. 5. Faith is not GOD, Christ, Wisdom, or the Holy Spirit, nor is it one of the invisible regions, their spirit, or the spirit of this world. Rather, it is a work of the Son of God, through which man learns to recognize God his Creator, Redeemer, and Sanctifier, and everything He has created and done for our benefit. This essential faith <note>The author uses "essential" here to mean the very substance or core of faith</note> is indeed lost in the essential contemplation of GOD, once evil is separated from good and the complete restoration of all things <note>original: "Wiederbringung aller Dinge"; a reference to the theological concept of Apocatastasis, the eventual salvation of all creation</note> has taken place. But faith is not thereby dissolved, nor does it pass away like human reason; instead, the Majestic Glory of GOD keeps it engulfed, so that it is eclipsed like a great, imperishable light by a Greater One—the Light of all lights. For when GOD shall be King upon His holy mountain and appear in glory before His elders, then even the sun of faith must feel shame, and its moon turn red with embarrassment. At that time, neither the light of the sun nor the moon will be necessary, because GOD and the Lamb illuminate everything.

§. 6. This precious faith, proven through all fires and trials, is certainly of a Majestic golden luster and surpasses the glory of the suns. The children of such a faith sing a song that no one can learn, except only the 144,000 who were re- <note>The text breaks here; the full thought likely refers to those "redeemed" or "bought" from the earth as described in Revelation 14:3</note>

§. 7. Therefore, those who fight the good fight of faith may rejoice and be glad in the Lord; yes, they may shout and exult: for joy in the Lord is our strength. This joy, however, is very far removed from the frivolous, vain joy of the world, the end of which is sorrow and sadness. Even if someone—according to the fatherly providence of God, who disciplines His beloved children and scourges everyone He accepts—should be led into a serious struggle with the darkness of unbelief and shameful <term>selfhood</term> <note>German: "Selbheit." A mystical term for egoism or the self-centered nature that separates a person from God.</note> which lies hidden under a harmful veil; let that person know that amidst the confluence of all kinds of adversities, the storm-winds of corrupted nature, and the impetuous waves of tribulations, *it is the Lord's time to glorify faith and to exalt its pure essence;* on such occasions, evil corruption is usually violently broken up. The spirit, life, essence, and powers of unbelief sometimes show themselves like desperate combatants and reckless soldiers who venture and attempt the extreme when they see their downfall and defeat before their eyes. Therefore, let no one be led astray into despondent faint-heartedness, much less follow the harmful impulses and abominable suggestions of unbelief, when its high-

§. 8. The end of faith <note>original: "Das Ende des Glaubens"; referring to 1 Peter 1:9, the goal or outcome of faith</note>, or several other such expressions of Holy Scripture, do not abolish faith as if they meant to say: essential faith shall be annihilated; no, rather they present to us its full measure and degree, which is the salvation of the soul, the mature manhood in Christ, or Christ’s full stature and height. For Jesus Christ, the author and finisher of faith, has brought faith to the highest perfection, made an end of all partial knowledge <note>original: "Stückwerks"; referring to 1 Corinthians 13:10</note>, and fulfilled all righteousness in His own person, which He is also willing to do and accomplish through the same in all His true followers.

# Chapter 10.

§. 2. O wonderful Love! You all-sufficient, highest Good, You can be everything at once, and yet You never cease to be, in infinite multiplicity, whatever each individual specifically requires. You break forth in infinite novelties and eternally changing beauties, yet You remain forever one unchanging Love. You are an eternal unity, and yet You are enjoyed in indescribably lovely diversity. You are a constant activity, and yet a stillness that rests perpetually. You are without beginning and end, yet You are the origin and completion of all things. You are incomprehensible, yet a true lover grasps and holds You completely fast with the arms of love, so that You become his eternal inheritance. You are almighty and invincible, yet You are conquered and overcome by a faithful and loyal lover. You are pure Spirit, and yet You become manifest in the flesh among Your beloved ones. You are the most high Majesty, yet so small and humble that You dwell in the least of Christ’s brothers. You are the Wisest and most Hidden, yet You associate with the little ones and reveal Yourself to the simple <note>original: "Unmündigen" — literally "minors" or "infants," referring to those with childlike faith</note>. # Chapter 10.

Hate evil with all your might; therefore, a person united with Satan and performing evil cannot be loved except with such hatred. And although their persons are capable of restoration <note>original: "Wiederbringung" – a reference to the doctrine of Apocatastasis or the eventual restoration of all things to God</note>, and thus as creatures of God must not be hated, they are nevertheless—for the sake of the evil with which they are united—to be regarded as heathens and tax collectors according to the Lord's own command, with whom one should have nothing to do. Indeed, in view of the antipathy, or the hatred between evil and good, Christ Himself is the death of death, the pestilence of hell, and the terror of all the godless.

§. 13. We must also learn to deny even our holy heavenly kin, so that neither life nor any creature may be able to separate us from the love of God, which is in Christ Jesus. Many a man who has overcome death stands in danger before life. Therefore, annihilation under the Cross <note>original: "Vernichtigung unterm Kreutze" – a mystical term for the total surrender of the self-will and ego</note> is inevitable. Through this, we learn to love the glorious sons and morning stars of God, the angels, the blessed, and the saints of God, the heavenly treasures and glories, our own life and precious soul—no longer for our own sake, but solely for the sake of God and Christ, within pure love and the original, most holy divine will.

# Of True Love.

<margin>Section 14.</margin> No one knows God, unless he loves Him, for love necessarily follows the true knowledge of the Highest Good; and God ignites the hearts of men through the goodness of His love. <margin>(a) Whoever knows God must also love Him.</margin> (a) But whoever loves anything more than God, and whoever loves without God, has either abandoned the love of God or has never truly known God.

# Chapter II.

->Concerning Self-Love, Selfhood, and Spiritual Pride.<-

<margin>Section 1.</margin> Corrupt **self-love**, which hides and presents itself under the appearance and colors of pure love, is so quick to mimic true love that an inexperienced person might well think it was of the right kind; it soars upward to God just like true love. Above all things, it desires to be blessed in time and eternity; therefore, it covers itself with hypocritical humility, godliness, internal and external purity, and with a desire for the glory of God. It is especially beautiful on the outside, but in its innermost being, it is fierce, seeking to murder and devour everything. It loves God for its own sake, and only as long as its own life remains unassailed. <margin>Note.</margin> It steals and robs God of everything. <note>The author warns that self-love is a parasitic imitation of divine love, seeking spiritual rewards for selfish gain rather than out of pure devotion.</note>

<summary>This page distinguishes between genuine divine love, which arises from true knowledge of God, and the deceptive nature of "self-love" which mimics piety for selfish ends. It warns that spiritual pride can hide behind a mask of humility and religious devotion.</summary>
<keywords>divine love, self-love, spiritual pride, knowledge of God, hypocrisy, humility, selfishness</keywords>

--- Page 104 ---
<meta>This page continues the discourse on the deceptive nature of self-love, following the previous chapter's introduction of the "spiritual beast."</meta>

# Chapter 11

<margin>Note Well.</margin>
When one head of this spiritual beast of self-love secretly robs God of His glory and kills one's neighbor, the other head presents itself as entirely flattering and hollow. Self-love does not easily allow itself to be convinced of its faults and sins; instead, out of fear of losing honor, reputation, and advantage, it covers up one head and puts forth another that shines with innocence. Self-love is "open-hearted" and "confiding" only when it wishes to elevate others in injustice for the sake of its own advantage, the better to hide itself. Its self-will, self-righteousness, its own deliberations, and its stubbornness always stand against God under a holy appearance.

<margin>
From this we have cause to constantly pray to God for the eyes of grace, that He might reveal this evil quality to us and desire to root it out from the very depths, because it seeks to nestle itself even into our best and otherwise God-pleasing actions—yes! It has permeated our entire being like a poison through and through. Blessed is he, therefore, who first truly recognizes it.
</margin>
The beast of self-love can hardly be rebuked or discovered without the Spirit of God. It praises its own will as wisdom from God; its own righteousness as true righteousness; its own deliberations, oppositions, and resistances of the "self" <note>original: "Eigenheit," referring to the ego or the state of being self-centered</note> as mere orderliness; its stubbornness as a holy strength and undaunted firmness. Self-love draws everything to its own advantage. It lusts to possess everything, and all of it under the appearance and pretext of humility, godliness, and other such virtues. In short: it loves itself above all things, and all other things for its own sake.

<center>§. 2.</center>

<term>Self-love</term> <note>Eigenliebe: the central vice discussed, an idolatrous focus on the self</note>, <term>Self-will</term> <note>Eigenwille: choosing one's own path over God's</note>, <term>Righteousness</term>, <term>Stubbornness</term>, <term>Spiritual Beast</term> <note>A metaphorical creature representing the multifaceted nature of sin</note>, <term>Humility</term>, <term>Godliness</term>, <term>Orderliness</term>

<summary>This page describes the deceptive "heads" of self-love, explaining how it masks its vices as virtues like wisdom and holiness to avoid detection. It emphasizes that self-love is a pervasive poison that can only be rooted out through divine grace and the Holy Spirit.</summary>

# On the Self-Love of Selfhood, etc.

§. 2. This powerful spiritual beast holds more dominion over man in his fallen state than God does. Those who are currently undergoing the process of <term>rebirth</term> <note>The Pietist concept of "Wiedergeburt," a spiritual transformation or being "born again" in Christ.</note> <margin>NB:</margin> must take great care <margin>(a) <insert>NB. they must certainly be on their guard</insert></margin> that the severed heads of self-love do not run back together or become reattached; for its power is greater than that of the hells. All unclean spirits and forces gladly allow themselves to be overcome by it; for it is a great harlot and a vessel of the most subtle malices. Indeed, in all her <term>Jezebellian</term> <note>Refers to Jezebel, the biblical queen associated with false prophets and seduction.</note> painted beauty, she is a member of the devil, who shamelessly blasphemes true love and seeks to murder it. This is because true love, with the light of God, exposes the gruesome darkness, and with its holy flames, incessantly drives away, burns, dissolves, and annihilates the poisonous, stinking dross of self-love.

The dragon-like monster and the two wild beasts described in Revelation <note>original: "Apoc." for Apocalypse/Revelation</note> chapter 12:3, chapter 13:1–8, 11–17, and chapter 17:3, depict and represent how the fallen man of <term>selfhood</term> <note>original: "Eigenheit," the state of being centered on one's own ego rather than God.</note> has filled himself, his soul, and its seven powers with the satanic forces—which burn poisonously with the <term>wrath-fire</term> <note>A term from Jacob Böhme’s theosophy, referring to the harsh, consuming fire of God's judgment or nature apart from His light.</note> and seek to devour and possess everything. He is filled with the animal-like, gluttonous capacities of this world that trample everything and seek to destroy; and with the false-prophetic qualities of the <term>reason-spirit</term> <note>original: "Vernunfts-Geist," often used by mystics to describe human intellect when it acts independently of divine revelation.</note> which mimics godliness, uses the grips of the dragon and the beast, and seduces the entire world...

# Chapter 12.
## Concerning man’s own righteousness and the true righteousness of God.

§. 1. All of our own righteousness is defiling; even if we could defend it as Job did against the accusations of friends and enemies, it nevertheless stands under the power of the <term>Leviathan and Behemoth</term><note>Biblical monsters from the Book of Job, here representing pride and the untamed ego.</note>, and together with them under the punitive justice of God. This is because such righteousness is produced and practiced by the self-loving people of <term>Selfhood</term><note>German: "Selbheit." A mystical term for the ego or the fallen state of being centered on oneself rather than God.</note>, who lie imprisoned in the jail of flesh and blood under the Devil and stand in union with him. Therefore, it cannot endure before the righteous God, but will be consumed like stubble and straw by the fire of His zeal; indeed, it stinks before Him like an unclean, filthy garment. ### §. 9. All confusion arises from the Historical and the Mystical Anti-Christ.

The Historical [Antichrist] strives in every way to annihilate the mystery of the Gospel and the independent essence of faith, along with everything that depends upon it. He seeks to fill the entire inhabited earth with empty ceremonies, endless laws, statutes, human ordinances, churches, academies, schools, the outward appearance of godliness, and similar powerless things. But the Mystical [Antichrist] wants to see Christ **for us** <note>The "Christ for us" refers to the substitutionary atonement—Christ's historical work on the cross.</note> completely cleared out of the way. He strives to abolish the Scripture and literal prayer, or at least to make all external benefits and the priceless gifts of God’s grace appear contemptible. In such a manner, he opposes and exalts himself against God, and sits as a god, both in the inner and outer temple of God (2 Thessalonians 2:4). One can well imagine that the Mystical wild beast—upon which the Arch-Whore, or Mother of Harlots, the great Babylon, rides most comfortably with all her cunning—is no less ostentatious than the external one. Both of them most powerfully promote the resurrection of the Mystical Jezebelian Beast-Prophet out of the cursed earth of Babylon. This prophet, in his two-horned lamb-like form, is most suited to deceive all those who dwell on the earth, <insert>along with the fact that they only want to have a "Christ for Us" so that the self-willed Old Man <note>A Pauline term for the unregenerate human nature.</note> within them does not have to die—but they will find themselves (Note Well!) deceived too late without the New Birth. Lord! open the eyes of all.</insert>

<summary>This page defines two forms of the Antichrist: the "Historical," which replaces true faith with empty church rituals, and the "Mystical," which rejects the historical Christ and Scripture in favor of a false internal spirituality. §. 17. In the innermost soul of fallen man, there exists the most subtle spiritual harlot, who embraces the hidden evil of selfhood <note>German: *Selbheit*; a term used in mystical theology to describe the ego or the self-centered nature that separates man from God</note>, both in herself and in others, with nothing but good-seeming mercy and flowery love. # On the Babylonian Mysteries, etc.

...equipped with three kinds of people or communities. 1) Those who seek to take possession of the Kingdom of God *without a change of heart <note>original: "Gemüths-Aenderung," referring to internal repentance or metanoia</note>, without the Cross, without the following of Christ, and without the death of the corrupted self-will*, or who seek to introduce a self-pleasing paradise and a rebirth on earth originating from the will of man.

With this, their *false Gospel and spirit of sorcery*, they enchant many noble souls who have not recognized the depths of Satan <note>A reference to Revelation 2:24</note>. It would therefore be good if God-loving souls would timely depart from such a bed of harlotry, wherein this mother gives birth to her modest Epicureans <note>Followers of Epicurus, here used pejoratively to mean those seeking worldly pleasure under the guise of religion</note>, who are very easily accepted as the representatives of the fallen old sects overflowing with all kinds of godlessness. Instead, just as Moses through faith, they should choose to suffer with the people of God—who, as chosen *silver, proven gold, genuine pearls, and precious stones, withstand the test*—and with the true brotherhood, keep the word of Jesus's patience, rather than having the enjoyment of sin, which is only for a short time.

2) Those who are to be regarded as harmonizing instruments of the Spirit of Lies, through which he seeks to bring forth his lying wonders. Their leaders and most prominent members are nothing but sorcerers and witches, who, along with all other diabolists <note>Those who follow or serve the devil</note>, [oppose] the outflow of the Holy

to seek reconciliation, which all people already possess before they believe; for Paul says, Romans 5:10, that we were reconciled to God while we were still enemies, and provided for in such a way that *Christ became a cause of eternal salvation to those who are obedient to him*; Hebrews 5:9, and who walk just *as he walked*. (b) My dear reader, stay a moment at these very important questions and do not pass over them so quickly. Whoever does not risk everything here will gain nothing there—therefore, be of the same mind as the Blessed Jesus Christ was.

presented, which is put on through walking with Christ, by faith, in conformity with His death, resurrection, and ascension. <margin>(a) Nothing is more foolish than to agree with the truth and yet not follow it—to know a good path and yet, out of laziness, refuse to walk it because it is somewhat thorny and narrow, even though **crowns** are to be obtained at the end of it; whereas the broad path, which is agreeable to the flesh, brings only **shame** as a reward at its end.</margin>

§. 5. Agreement with the truth *without the practice* of it is of no use. The days of a godly person are like those of a day-laborer, and in the hasty stretching of faith toward the true rest of God, false peace will utterly vanish. Whoever wishes to enjoy, taste, and see the peace which the world cannot give, and the Spirit which it cannot receive, their faith always proves itself through their works; for just as faith without works is dead, so also are works without faith sinful, stained, and worthless before God. # On the Confession of Truth

...rational mouth-chatter from the memory is a human work that merely itches the ears. <note>A reference to 2 Timothy 4:3, describing people who seek teachers who tell them only what they want to hear.</note>

§. 2. What one knows in truth, one can also confess in faith. What one expects in faith is certainly a secure hope. However, one must learn to know and view things as they are at their foundation, and take good heed of their fundamental qualities. Generally, however, these things are accepted by people as they appear to be, and *not as they actually are*; thus they miserably deceive themselves and others. But true love neither believes nor hopes in the mixtures flowing from false reasons and appearances, because they are nothing other than painted lies and deceptions, or the precise joining together of contrary things.

<margin>(*) But a mouth-conversation brought forth from mere feeling <note>original: "Gefülliße," likely referring to superficial sentiment</note> does not touch the ground of the soul, as is proven by the dead preachers; despite their much preaching, the world nevertheless becomes ever worse and more wicked.</margin>

§. 3. Believers are not accustomed to forcing their confessions upon consciences with violence, but rather they speak and bear witness to the truth in faith. <margin>as Babylon <note>original: "Babel"; a common Radical Pietist term for the established, corrupt church systems</note> does.</margin> Whoever is of God hears the word of God. But the unbelievers and those uncircumcised in heart and ears *always resist the Holy Spirit, and Christ is to them a sign which is spoken against. For He has come to kindle a fire upon the earth*, which attacks, dissolves, and annihilates all corruption.

§. 4. Everyone who confesses Christ in Christ Jesus, let him hasten with eagle's wings to the wilderness, <note>A mystical reference to Revelation 12:14, symbolizing the soul's flight from worldly corruption to a place of spiritual refuge.</note>

<summary>This page contrasts genuine spiritual confession with superficial "mouth-chatter," arguing that true faith must be grounded in reality rather than appearances. It criticizes established church systems (Babylon) for using force and producing "dead preachers" who fail to transform the world.</summary>

<keywords>confession of truth, dead preachers, Babylon, Holy Spirit, spiritual wilderness, Radical Pietism, inner word, hypocrisy</keywords>

# Chapter 19.
## Concerning Prayer.

§. 1. According to one's age in Christ <note>The author refers to stages of spiritual maturity rather than physical age.</note>, prayer is also found and changed. The Spirit of prayer proves itself according to the capacity of its instrument. Small children cry out in their own way; the youths call out from their strength; the sick whimper, and so forth. But in and among those perfectly crowned, all-conquering heroes of faith, all powers are joined together in a harmonizing, divine play of strings. These have become, through the birth from above, the true owners of their souls and renewed natures, wherein they sing the song of Moses, the servant of God, and the song of the Lamb <note>A reference to Revelation 15:3, symbolizing the union of the Old and New Testaments in victory.</note>.

<margin>Note Well.</margin>

All words in prayer that do not spring forth from this ground of faith are spoken in vain, no matter how precious and

## Chapter 20.

### Concerning True Prudence and Freedom, Together with the True and False Nothingness.

§. 1. One should have nothing to do with poisonous envy, because the riches of wisdom ought to be distributed without being buried away. Yet, let no one be so foolish as to pour out his spirit entirely. *The sanctuary must be preserved and so shrouded that the dogs cannot scent it out. The pearls shall be locked away and presented so cautiously to those who love God, that the swine may not trample them with their feet.* <note>A reference to Matthew 7:6; the author emphasizes that spiritual truths must be protected from those who would mock or misuse them.</note> Truth is always right to those who understand it, and blameless to all who are willing to accept it. # Chapter 21

By this, a person is led to neither allow themselves to be shaped nor to understand anything, even though it is written: "Stir up the gift of God which is—NB:—in you."
</margin>
The imagined and infallible "Nothingness" always responds with something unusual; but a false "Nothingness" leads a person into many unrecognized difficulties, until finally, through the Cross, he loses humility, and through faith, he loses all true knowledge of God. Whereupon the will demands and insists upon having an "unrecognized God" for itself and others.

§. 4. The smallest among Christ's smallest brothers is closest to Christ, or the disciple of the teaching whom the Lord especially loves. For what is highly esteemed among men is an abomination in the sight of God. <note>Luke 16:15</note>

# Chapter 22.

§. 4. The firebrand of the Whore <note>A reference to the "Whore of Babylon" from the Book of Revelation, symbolizing worldly corruption and false spirituality.</note> brings exceedingly great sadness to the kings of the earth; it also causes the many seafarers—those who sail in the moisture of melancholy and trade with confused senses—to weep mightily. These dark and bitter spirits of mourning, who manifest as kings and merchants within the nature of the soul, weep bitterly when they lose all their pleasure and profit through the firebrand of the Whore. <margin>Note:</margin> With their sadness, they infect the inner earth of mankind. Therefore, it is highly necessary to withdraw from such mourning and cares, and much rather to harmonize with Heaven. Heaven rejoices greatly over the thorough destruction of this godless Whore, who has always been a shedder of the blood of Christ, his Apostles, and Prophets. Heaven knows well that the nature of the soul must be purified in such a way with a sharp fire of cleansing, and that the whorish Babylon or Jericho <note>Jericho is used here as a type of the "fallen city" that must be overcome before entering the promised land of spiritual peace.</note> must be utterly banished from it.

§. 4. When **Jesus** is conceived in a spiritual manner, it happens within a sorrowful human spirit through the overshadowing power of the Most High. If **Jesus** is born, it happens from a virgin in a bitterly sorrowful and contrite heart, out of a suffering will which experiences birth-pains and labors within **God’s** mercy. If **Jesus** is to grow, it happens by increasing in wisdom, in physical stature, and in favor—or grace—with **God** and men. If he is to be persecuted, it happens by King Herod, the Pharisees, and the Jews. When he is to be tempted, it happens in the wilderness, upon the pinnacle—or the highest wing-gallery—of the temple, and upon a very high mountain by the Devil. §. 5. The faithful soul has two arms: with one, she embraces her *Jesus*, and with the other, she surrenders herself entirely to His providence and will. Jesus also hides Himself at times within the faithful soul, not unlike a dear mother who has stepped away from her child, but because of the child's incessant crying and calling, is compelled to take it up in her arms again, because the child will be satisfied by no one else. ## Chapter 23.

->Concerning a true Christian and their godly conduct before GOD.<-

<margin>
(a) I will deal with the One God, both in solitude and in community, and hold to the One, and what is not God, I will leave, flee, etc.
</margin>

§. 1 A beginner Christian, having been seized by grace, hourly turns and draws his mind, his will, his senses, desires, thoughts, and strivings—amidst the inner sighing of the spirit—away from everything that is not God, into himself; diligently seeking with all his might for the pearl hidden in the soul, which is Christ in us; or for the heavenly mustard seed; the heavenly leaven; the kingdom of God within us; the eternal light, life, and love; the imperishable seed according to the Word that was in the beginning, and is very near to us in our heart and in our mouth; according to the true light that enlightens all people who come into the world; but

<insert>
Note: A fiery soul <note>A soul filled with spiritual zeal or passion</note> who does not seek to diligently and earnestly practice the aforementioned turning away of the mind from everything that is not God, and the constant penetrating into Him, will not be able to attain any inner powers, nor find the pearl.
</insert>

§. 12. The powers of the established spirits are so artistically interwoven and preserved by Wisdom <note>referring to Sophia, or Divine Wisdom</note> that no one is able to search out the ground of their joining. The multiplicity of these spirits, or their entire being, is sealed and bound together in *Unity*; they are as incorruptible living stones in the living building of the living **GOD**. Thus, the manifoldness is put on as an adornment to the simplicity; for *Unity* is the ground or origin of multiplicity, and *multiplicity* is an ornament of *Unity*.

§. 13. Among the sincere and true followers of Jesus, the mixed activity of the soul always ends in suffering, and suffering ends in death. Likeness to the death of Jesus brings with it the complete resurrection, and the perfect ascension brings an apostolic descent, or a mission from Christ. Vain reason is so climbed up and scattered in its own multiplicity that a person cannot lose their confused nature without this process; instead, simple truths and divine mysteries appear to reason as foolish or nonsensical. Yet, in and of themselves, these truths are truly so childlike, pure, and simple that the reason of the learned—highly esteemed by the world and parading in vanities—must itself become a fool with all its confused sciences and give up the ghost <note>original: "den Geist aufgeben," meaning both to die and to surrender one's own spirit</note> before one can fundamentally understand what the children of wisdom speak, and learn the new song, or the song of Moses the servant of God, and the song of the Lamb. <note>A reference to Revelation 15:3, symbolizing the song of deliverance and victory.</note>

<margin>Note Well.</margin>
<margin>≠</margin>

Thus, when the eye of the mind is darkened, and the tempter moves beneath the vapors of the mortal body
<insert>≠ when the flesh becomes too strong and too heavy, it gains dominion over the spirit and causes a swelling in the gateway of the mind, followed by great darkness.</insert>

# On the Trials of the Believers

It is a blessed work, but to exalt oneself over it is a characteristic of the Devil. **God** forgets none of all His work, but rather sustains everything with His powerful word. To rebuke and admonish one's falling and failing neighbor out of love (at the right time and upon good opportunity, according to a true, tested, and living impulse), without contempt for him or self-exaltation, is laudable and essential; but to seek advantage, honor, and elevation through it is **Antichristian** <note>original: "Antichristisch"; referring to a spirit that opposes the humble nature of Christ</note>. Let everyone be quick to hear and slow to speak, and by no means run before he is equipped with the power of the Holy Spirit, which teaches, sends, and orders all things in spiritual matters. To judge frivolously out of passion without the impulse of love is damnable; and to love frivolously out of passion without justice is abominable; to be just without love is terrifying (a).

# Concerning the Trials of the Believers

A miraculous fast belongs to Moses, Elijah, John, and our Savior Christ, as well as to those to whom it will be given during certain unavoidable occurrences and changes of times. If you are animalistic <note>original: "thierlich," referring to living by base instincts rather than spirit</note>, then become rational; if you are rational, then flee to the grace <margin>m.</margin> in your heart; for an animalistic man is a slave and servant to his lusts and desires. A rational man governs them as well as he can, but grace kills, burns, and drives out both branch and root. A Christian alone can be full in hunger, and hungry in abundance, until the essence of Love fills and satisfies both soul and body. The depth of the fallen soul, which formerly was a garden of all delight and abundance, is now dry and rocky, a container of ghosts and wild animals, where bread becomes expensive before the Manna <note>The miraculous food provided by God to the Israelites in the desert</note> rains down, and where the anguish becomes heavy before the lily greens and blooms. Babel <note>Babylon, representing worldly confusion and pride</note> is a tower that did not exist before the Fall; many take leaps of joy from it and fall, but it must fall itself, if you remain constant and faithful. Around Jerusalem are mountains, from whence the earth is to be viewed as magnificent, where pleasure appears to be Love, the images appear vivid, and the creatures appear to be **GOD**; then fast according to your ability. # On the Trials of Believers

O you hidden deceiver! You must be revealed with all your concealed cunning. When the wisdom of God catches you in your malice, then you must be helpful in destroying yourself. When you strike with fists, revile, and persecute, you awaken the justice of God against yourself and your slave, and drive the <term>new man</term><note>A Pauline concept referring to the regenerated spiritual nature of a believer.</note> into the depths of God's mercy. # Chapter 26.

## Concerning the Answer and Internal Address of God, and How One Attains It.

§. 1. Urim and Thummim <note>Hebrew: "Lights and Perfections." These were sacred objects used by the High Priest in the Old Testament to determine God's will.</note> bring with them the true sacrifice and incense, which constantly flows forth and rises up from the essential white and red light. **Light and Perfection contain within themselves the infallible oral answer and internal address of God.**

§. 2. It is true, we must pass from the outer through the inner into the innermost; however, we cannot rightly enter the outer unless we have first reached the inner. Even less can we enter the outermost unless it happens through the innermost. For the inner is greater than the outer, and the innermost grasps and encompasses the outermost within itself. Thus, the length, breadth, height, and depth of the inner and innermost extend further, higher, deeper, longer, and wider than the outer and the very outermost. Whoever understands this fundamentally knows how the Holy and True One, who holds the Key of David <note>A biblical reference to Christ's authority to open what no one can shut (Revelation 3:7).</note>, opens the seals. Generally, everyone can gather at least this much from it: that without Christ—

## Chapter 27.

### Concerning the imageless shield against all evil thoughts.

§. 1. After we have reached—in the spirit and within the essence of our spirit—the depth, the height, the length, the breadth, the circumference, the center, the innermost, and the outermost, so that Jerusalem <note>A common mystical symbol for the state of divine peace and the soul's true home.</note> is constantly characterized by our whole being; and yet, should one or more images of Babel <note>original: "Babels-Bilder"; referring to confusing, worldly, or idolatrous mental distractions that oppose the clarity of Jerusalem.</note>—of whatever kind they may be—nevertheless appear in order to hinder the imageless, essential communion and walk with or before God, then one must flee into his fortress. One must be as swift as a roe or a young stag to occupy the central heights or mountains, and from there, with the eye of the soul and of faith, look through such images; then they shall fall and break apart. For then they come to stand between faith and God, where no images can endure; instead, all things are beheld essentially and presently, exactly as they are, in the clear mirror of Wisdom.

# Of the Word of God.
<page-num>261</page-num>

Wisdom is poured out and released over all His work, and *His imperishable Spirit is in everything that He has made through His Word.* <note>Reference to Wisdom of Solomon 12:1: "For thy incorruptible Spirit is in all things."</note> His wise, all-searching, all-encompassing, and understanding Spirit is an immeasurable sea of grace, justice, love, and wisdom. Therefore, if anyone lacks wisdom, let him ask. <note>A reference to James 1:5.</note>

§. 3. Regarding the God-loving, well-known Catholic and Protestant writers <term>Scribenten: authors or scholars</term>, they are not pure or liberated from their national churches, monastic life, human ordinances, and the like.

§. 4. When reading books, it should be noted that while all things are permitted to a believer, not all things are beneficial. Therefore, one must *ask God for wisdom* in order to choose the good, and to reject not only the evil but also unnecessary things.

§. 5. Why, or to what extent, is the Holy Scripture infallible? Answer: 1) Because it was dictated by the Holy Spirit. 2) Insofar as it is free from the small errors of translators, scribes, or others of that kind, which nevertheless cannot diminish its value and high esteem; for the Spirit and the life always follow His work as long as it remains unadulterated and undistorted. Of the godless it is rightly said: Why do you recite my statutes, and take my covenant into your mouth, since you *hate discipline* and cast my words behind you? <note>original: "Warum erzählest du meine Einsetzungen, und nimmest meinen Bund in deinem Mund, so du doch Zucht haßest..." — a translation of Psalm 50:16-17.</note> There will...

# Chapter 30.
## On Labor.

§. 1. In Jesus Christ <note>original: "Christo JESU"</note> we have an infallible pattern and model for how we ought to properly conduct ourselves in all things; the examples of others may be followed only insofar as they have followed and still follow Him, but not a single step further, neither to the right nor to the left. When the disciples of Christ saw that they had failed in their self-made hopes, opinions, and thoughts regarding Christ and His kingdom, they began again to practice their former trade, until they were appointed as fishers of men <note>A reference to the disciples returning to fishing after the crucifixion, before the resurrection appearances (John 21).</note> through a renewed calling. The ways of the Lord are wonderful; He does not overwhelm us with statutes, but often lets us run until we are weary and exhausted, falling upon the blessed cornerstone <note>original: "Eckstein"; a biblical metaphor for Christ as the foundation of faith.</note> and becoming blessedly contrite. From such softened, mellowed, and humbled hearts, He afterwards forms, through His Spirit, vessels for the execution of His work. # Chapter 35.

When human ordinances had swallowed everything up and the Jewish Temple had become a den of murderous robbers, *the Lord Himself came* and established the New Covenant, in which the ceremonies of Moses reached their end. In the same way, and even more so, one can imagine that all human ordinances, corrupted actions, and ceremonies must sink into their *nothingness* at the appearance of Jesus Christ—when the entire "man of sin" <note>A reference to the biblical concept of the fallen human nature or the Antichrist, see 2 Thessalonians 2:3</note> comes to an end and the eternal Gospel shall be proclaimed. Then, the *figurative essence of the New Testament* shall emerge in its immutable, incorruptible, and divine state, under which the internal and external Antichrist <note>The author distinguishes between the "external" Antichrist in the world and the "internal" Antichrist within the human soul</note> with all his adversities shall be utterly defeated, and the royal reign of Jesus Christ shall be eternally established. At that time, the believers shall no longer be baptized in a figurative manner into elemental water; rather, according to the instruction already given by the Lord in Matthew 28:18-20, the messengers of Christ will *immerse* those who have been made disciples—without the slightest addition—into the Name of the Father, the Son, and the Holy Spirit, *which is the divine nature* or the living streams of water of the Spirit, which flow from the fullness of Christ out of their innermost being.
